- Summary: Extended the Material/Product/Printer data-integrity chain to the `PrintJob` production record — the P0 "Stabilize Production Data" core. Added `[Range(0, …)]` annotations to the user-editable numeric fields on the `PrintJob` entity (`FilamentUsedGrams`, `FilamentUsedMeters`, `EstimatedTimeMinutes`, `ActualTimeMinutes`, `ReportedCost`), which the `PrintJobs/Edit` form binds directly with no prior numeric guard. This closes a real integrity gap: for any non-`Completed` status the stock service's `filamentUsedGrams <= 0` check is skipped, so a negative grams/cost/time value would persist freely and corrupt dashboard cost/time aggregation plus the computed `CalculatedMaterialCost` (grams × costPerKg, unguarded).
- Timestamp: 2026-06-30
- Files changed: `src/ThreeDManager.Domain/Entities/PrintJob.cs`, `tests/ThreeDManager.Tests/PrintJobControllerIntegrationTests.cs`, `docs/context/tasks_diary.md`
- Validation run: `dotnet build ThreeDManager.slnx` passed with 0 warnings and 0 errors; focused `dotnet test tests\ThreeDManager.Tests\ThreeDManager.Tests.csproj --no-build --filter EditPrintJob_RejectsNegativeFilamentGrams_WithoutPersistingChange` passed with 1 test; `dotnet test ThreeDManager.slnx --no-build` passed with 41 tests; `dotnet ef migrations has-pending-model-changes` reported no model changes (annotations are validation-only, no migration needed); the new MVC integration test posts `FilamentUsedGrams = -5.00` to `/PrintJobs/Edit/{id}`, asserts the rendered page shows "O filamento usado (g) não pode ser negativo." and that a fresh `AppDbContext` scope confirms `FilamentUsedGrams` stayed `12.45` and `ReportedCost` stayed `2.50` (the rejected post persisted nothing); live local-Postgres boot smoke on `http://localhost:5042` returned HTTP 200 for `/PrintJobs` and `/PrintJobs/Edit/{id}`, and the rendered Edit form emitted `data-val-range="O filamento usado (g) não pode ser negativo." data-val-range-min="0"` plus the matching `data-val-range-min="0"` on `EstimatedTimeMinutes`.

Added a nullable, non-negative `MinimumStockQuantity` (int) to `Product`, mirroring `Material.MinimumStockGrams`/its existing low-stock-badge convention exactly. Wired entity → migration → `ProductsController.Edit` (now also copies the field onto the tracked entity) → all CRUD views: `Products/Create` and `Products/Edit` collect the field, `Products/Details` adds an "Estoque mínimo" row and a "Status estoque" badge (`Baixo estoque`/`Ok`/`Sem alerta`, same guard as Material: both current and minimum stock must be present, and low stock is `current <= minimum`), and `Products/Index` adds the same two columns per row. The `PrintJob`-completion stock-deduction half of the prior recommendation was deliberately deferred: `PrintJob` has no "units produced" concept today (it models one production run, not a unit count), so wiring that would require first deciding and modeling a units-produced field — a separate, less narrow batch — rather than reusing this one.

This preserved all 4 pre-existing `PrintJobStockServiceTests` cases (which don't set `ProductId`) and the integration-test suite unchanged. `UnitsProduced <= 0` with a `ProductId` set still fails validation, matching the material pattern's required-quantity check.

## 2026-07-11 (continuous run)

- Summary: Live inspection first confirmed the prior checkpoint's carried-forward "no linked production yet, but not eligible to generate one" recommendation for `Error`-status imports was already implemented (`PrintImportsController.GetProcessAvailability`, added in earlier `feat: harden print import retry guidance` / `feat: clarify import recovery actions` commits, already renders a distinct "Reimporte um arquivo compatível para seguir." hint and hides the retry button on both `PrintImports/Index` and `Details` whenever an import has no raw content or no matching parser); no code change was needed there. Instead, closed a real Phase 1 stock-visibility gap: `Materials/Index` showed a per-row "Baixo estoque" badge but had no way to filter the list down to only low-stock materials, unlike `PrintImports/Index`'s existing `status=error`/`status=uploaded` filter pattern. `MaterialsController.Index` now accepts an optional `stockStatus` query parameter; when `stockStatus=low`, the list is filtered server-side to materials whose `CurrentStockGrams` is at or below `MinimumStockGrams` (both must be set), reusing the same comparison the view already used for the per-row badge. The view gained a "Todos" / "Baixo estoque (N)" filter button pair mirroring `PrintImports/Index`'s styling, plus a filtered empty-state message. Presentation/filtering-only; no schema, migration, or stock mutation logic changed.
- Also fixed a real, pre-existing test-infrastructure isolation bug uncovered while validating the new filter: `ThreeDManagerWebFactory` set a unique `Testing:DatabaseName` per instance, but `Program.cs`'s EF Core `UseInMemoryDatabase(databaseName)` call was not actually isolating by that name in practice — running two or more integration tests in the same process caused every "PLA Preto" material ever seeded by any prior test in the run to accumulate into what was assumed to be a fresh, empty per-test database (confirmed by dumping the tracked rows mid-test: a single test's own "isolated" database contained 38 stale `PLA Preto` rows left over from every earlier test in the sequential run). This was silently tolerated by existing tests because none of them asserted an exact cross-test row count the way the new low-stock filter test needed to; it was pure luck of assertion looseness, not real isolation. Fixed by having `ThreeDManagerWebFactory.ConfigureWebHost` explicitly re-register `AppDbContext` via `ConfigureTestServices`, pinning each factory instance to its own `InMemoryDatabaseRoot` object (in addition to the existing per-instance database name), which guarantees isolation independent of any configuration-timing behavior. This only touches test infrastructure (`tests/ThreeDManager.Tests/ThreeDManagerWebFactory.cs`); no production code changed.
- Timestamp: 2026-07-11 (continuous run)
- Files changed: `src/ThreeDManager.Web/Controllers/MaterialsController.cs`, `src/ThreeDManager.Web/Views/Materials/Index.cshtml`, `tests/ThreeDManager.Tests/ThreeDManagerWebFactory.cs`, `tests/ThreeDManager.Tests/PrintJobControllerIntegrationTests.cs`, `docs/context/tasks_diary.md`.
- Validation run: environment precheck passed (`threedmanager-db` was `Exited` at run start — started via `docker compose up -d db` and confirmed with `pg_isready`); new focused test `MaterialsIndex_FiltersLowStockMaterials_WhenStockStatusLowRequested` (seeds one low-stock material at 120g/200g minimum and one healthy material at 1000g/200g minimum, asserts the unfiltered view shows `Baixo estoque (1)` and both material names, and the `?stockStatus=low` view shows `Baixo estoque (1)` with only the low-stock material's name present) initially failed nondeterministically when run alongside any other test in the suite due to the InMemory isolation bug described above — after the `ThreeDManagerWebFactory` fix, `dotnet build ThreeDManager.slnx` passed with 0 warnings and 0 errors, and full `dotnet test ThreeDManager.slnx --no-build` passed with all 81 tests (up from 80), confirmed stable across three consecutive full runs. Live local-PostgreSQL smoke via Claude Preview tools on `http://127.0.0.1:5042/Materials`: baseline showed "Baixo estoque (0)" (the one seeded material's stock, 987,55 g, was above its 200 g minimum); temporarily set `CurrentStockGrams = 50` via `docker exec … psql`, reloaded, and confirmed the counter updated to "Baixo estoque (1)" with the row's status badge showing "Baixo estoque"; navigated to `/Materials?stockStatus=low` and confirmed only that one material row rendered. Reverted `CurrentStockGrams` back to `987.55` via `psql`, reloaded the filtered view, and confirmed it rendered "Baixo estoque (0)" and the new "Nenhum material em baixo estoque no momento." empty-state message; a final read-only query confirmed the material's stock matched the original seeded value exactly. The dev server was stopped after verification.
- Blockers: none. DB container was down at run start (normal after idle periods); started cleanly via `docker compose up -d db`.

Separately, since the `ThreeDManagerWebFactory` isolation fix was scoped narrowly to unblock this batch's own test, it would be worth a quick sanity pass confirming no other existing integration test was silently relying on the old (broken) cross-test data accumulation for a passing assertion — all 81 tests currently pass cleanly under the fixed isolation, but this was not exhaustively audited test-by-test.

## 2026-07-12 (continuous run, part 4)

- Summary: Closed the "per-printer estimated queue clear time" recommendation from the prior checkpoint. The existing "Fila de produção por impressora" card on `Dashboard/Index` and the "Fila de produção" column on `Printers/Index` already showed queue depth (count + total estimated minutes) but gave no sense of *when* a printer would actually be free. Added `EstimatedClearAt` (DateTime, UTC) to both `PrinterQueueSummaryViewModel` (used by `Printers/Index`) and `DashboardPrinterQueueViewModel` (used by `Dashboard/Index`), computed in each controller as a single `DateTime.UtcNow` snapshot per request plus that printer's `QueuedEstimatedTimeMinutes` — i.e. "if this printer runs its queued Imported/Planned jobs back-to-back starting now, it clears at this time." `Dashboard/Index.cshtml`'s queue table gained a "Previsão de fila livre" column (`dd/MM/yyyy HH:mm`, `.ToLocalTime()`, matching the existing `CreatedAt`/`ImportedAt` formatting convention used everywhere else in the app), and `Printers/Index.cshtml`'s queue cell gained a "Livre em dd/MM HH:mm" hint under the existing count/time/warning-badge line. Purely additive and derived from already-persisted `PrintJob.EstimatedTimeMinutes`/`Status`/`PrinterId`; no schema, migration, or queue-grouping logic changed.

## 2026-07-12 (continuous run, part 6)

- Summary: Fixed a real audit-trail gap found via code survey (not the diary, which can go stale): `MaterialsController.Edit` has always written a `MaterialStockMovement` ("Ajuste manual realizado no cadastro do material.") whenever the plain Edit form changed `CurrentStockGrams`, but the structurally identical `ProductsController.Edit` never wrote a matching `ProductStockMovement` when `StockQuantity` changed through the same form — only the dedicated `AdjustStock` flow logged an audit row for products. This silently let a product's stock be changed with zero audit trail via `/Products/Edit`, unlike materials. Mirrored the exact `MaterialsController.Edit` pattern into `ProductsController.Edit`: capture `StockQuantity` before the field copy, and if it changed, add a `ProductStockMovement` with `MovementType = "ManualAdjustment"`, `QuantityUnits` as the delta, `StockBeforeUnits`/`StockAfterUnits`, and the note "Ajuste manual realizado no cadastro do produto." Presentation/audit-only; no schema, migration, or view change (the "Ajuste manual" rendering already existed on `Products/Details` for `AdjustStock`-originated movements).

## 2026-07-12 (continuous run, part 10)

Root-caused via the exact `Expected/Actual` string diffs in `dotnet test` output, then fixed by renaming the new parameter to `filterPrinterId` throughout (controller, views, hidden fields, tests) to make it unambiguous with the entity property — confirmed by the full suite passing again at 84/84 with no other collisions.

## 2026-07-12 (remote-use preparation, alpha authentication)

- Summary: Added the first fail-closed authentication boundary for alpha use. Every MVC route now requires an authenticated cookie by default; only `Account/Login` is anonymous. The single alpha operator credential is supplied through `AlphaAccess__Username` and `AlphaAccess__Password`, never stored in source control. Successful login issues a 12-hour sliding, HTTP-only, SameSite=Lax cookie with the `Operator` role; the shared navigation renders the signed-in username and a CSRF-protected POST logout action. Invalid credentials return one generic error, and local-only `returnUrl` normalization prevents external redirect injection. Outside the automated `Testing` environment, startup fails explicitly when either credential is missing. No database/schema change was needed.

## 2026-07-12 (continuous run, part 12)

- Summary: Fixed a real production-failure-tracking display bug found via code survey (remote ingress/Windows-service work from the prior checkpoint requires elevated manual execution and is out of scope for this run). `PrintJobs/Details.cshtml` rendered its "Motivo da falha" row unconditionally whenever `PrintJob.FailureReason` was non-empty, with no check on the job's *current* `Status`. Since `PrintJobsController.Edit` POST never clears `FailureReason` when a job's status is corrected away from `Failed` (e.g. a reprint succeeds and the operator marks it `Completed`), the Details page kept showing the old failure reason on a production that was no longer failed — misleading, and inconsistent with `PrintJobs/Index.cshtml` and `Dashboard/Index.cshtml`, which already only show their "Motivo: ..." line when `PrintJobStatus.Normalize(job.Status) == PrintJobStatus.Failed`. Gated the same `dt`/`dd` pair in `Details.cshtml` behind that identical status check, matching the existing pattern exactly. Chose to hide the stale value rather than clear the underlying `FailureReason` column on status change, consistent with the repo's standing "preserve raw/audit data, don't destructively transform it" stance — the reason a job previously failed remains queryable in the database even after the status is corrected, it just no longer surfaces as an active failure reason on the current Details view. Presentation-only; no controller, schema, migration, or stock/cost logic changed.
- Timestamp: 2026-07-12 (continuous run, part 12)
- Files changed: `src/ThreeDManager.Web/Views/PrintJobs/Details.cshtml`, `tests/ThreeDManager.Tests/PrintJobControllerIntegrationTests.cs`, `docs/context/tasks_diary.md`.

Extended the existing `EditPrintJob_ToFailed_WithReason_PersistsAndShowsOnDetails` test with a second edit round-trip: after asserting the `Failed`-status Details page shows "Motivo da falha" and the reason text, the test now re-submits the same Edit form with `Status=Completed` while the `FailureReason` field still carries its old value (simulating a stale browser form), then asserts the redirect succeeds, the DB row shows `Status="Completed"` with `FailureReason` still equal to the original text (data preserved, not cleared), and the redirected Details page contains neither "Motivo da falha" nor the reason text — passed (1/1); `dotnet build ThreeDManager.slnx` passed with 0 warnings and 0 errors; full `dotnet test ThreeDManager.slnx --no-build` passed with all 86 tests (same count — existing test extended, no new `[Fact]` needed). Live local-PostgreSQL smoke: started `dotnet run --project src/ThreeDManager.Web/ThreeDManager.Web.csproj --no-build --launch-profile http --urls http://127.0.0.1:5062` with temporary process-only `AlphaAccess__Username`/`AlphaAccess__Password`; logged in via `curl` with a cookie jar and extracted antiforgery token; inserted a temporary `Imported` print job (`smoke-failurereason-details.gcode`) against the existing seeded product/printer/material via `docker exec … psql`; edited it to `Status=Failed` with `FailureReason="Entupimento do bico durante smoke test"` through the real `/PrintJobs/Edit` form and confirmed `/PrintJobs/Details/{id}` rendered both "Motivo da falha" and the reason text; then edited the same job to `Status=Completed` (submitting the identical stale `FailureReason` value, as a browser would if the field weren't cleared client-side) and confirmed the redirected Details page rendered neither string, while a read-only `psql` query confirmed the row's `FailureReason` column still held the original text with `Status="Completed"` — proving the fix hides stale display without destroying the underlying data. The temporary row was deleted and a read-only query confirmed zero `smoke-failurereason-details.gcode` rows remained. The background dev-server task was stopped after verification.
- Blockers: none. Noted for future runs: extracting the antiforgery token from a rendered page with a single `grep -o ... | sed ...` pipeline is unsafe on any page that renders more than one `@Html.AntiForgeryToken()` form (e.g. `_Layout.cshtml`'s logout form plus the page's own form) — it silently concatenates both matches into one corrupted value and every POST fails with an empty `400 Bad Request` that looks identical to the controller's own `id != printJob.Id` `BadRequest()` guard. Pipe through `head -1` before the `sed` extraction on any page reached while authenticated (i.e. every page except `Account/Login`, which has no layout).

## 2026-07-12 (continuous run, part 15)

- Summary: Centralized the manual stock-adjustment mode literals (`"Add"`, `"Remove"`, `"Set"`), the last remaining un-centralized magic-string set in the stock modules, following the same pattern established for `PrintJobStatus` (part earlier), `StockMovementType` write-side (part 13), and `StockMovementTypePresentation` read-side (part 14). These three strings — the UI selection on the AdjustStock form that maps to a *signed* stock movement quantity — were duplicated across six sites: both stock-adjustment view-model defaults (`MaterialStockAdjustmentViewModel`, `ProductStockAdjustmentViewModel`, each defaulting `AdjustmentType = "Add"`), both controller `switch` blocks (`MaterialsController.AdjustStock` POST, `ProductsController.AdjustStock` POST), and both `<option value="...">` sets in the `Materials/AdjustStock` and `Products/AdjustStock` views. The concrete risk being closed: a typo at any single site (a controller `case`, a view option value, or the view-model default) would not fail to compile — it would silently fall through to the controller's `default` "Tipo de ajuste inválido." branch (or, if the view option drifted from the controller case, reject a legitimately-selected mode), a fail-visible-but-avoidable behavior. Added a `StockAdjustmentType` static constants class in the existing `ThreeDManager.Web.ViewModels` namespace (deliberately in the Web layer, not Domain, because `AdjustmentType` is a transient UI concept that is never persisted — unlike `StockMovementType`, which lives in `ThreeDManager.Domain.Entities` because its values are written to the movement ledger) with `Add`/`Remove`/`Set` constants, and replaced all six literal sites with references to it. Both controllers already `using ThreeDManager.Web.ViewModels`; added `@using ThreeDManager.Web.ViewModels` to the two AdjustStock views (they previously only fully-qualified their `@model`). ## 2026-07-12 (continuous run, part 16)

- Summary: Added a per-unit production cost line ("Custo por unidade") to the PrintJobs `Details` view — a purely additive Phase 2 (costing) increment that reuses the already-computed `estimatedTotalProductionCost` (material + machine + packaging) and the persisted `PrintJob.UnitsProduced`, with no schema change. The view already surfaced the whole-job total cost, margin, and suggested price, but for batch prints (a plate producing several identical units) it exposed no per-unit economics; the new line divides the estimated total by `UnitsProduced` (`Math.Round(total / UnitsProduced, 2)`) and renders it with a "custo total estimado X ÷ N unidades" breakdown. The block is gated on `Model.UnitsProduced > 1`, so single-unit jobs (the default, `UnitsProduced = 1`) render byte-identically to before — every existing costing/margin/suggested-price integration assertion is unaffected. Deliberately did NOT touch the "Margem estimada"/"Preço de venda sugerido" math: both currently compare a per-unit `Product.SalePrice` against the whole-job `estimatedTotalProductionCost`, which is only correct for single-unit jobs and understates margin when `UnitsProduced > 1` (present identically in both `PrintJobs/Details` and `PrintJobs/Index`). Reinterpreting margin as per-unit changes displayed numbers based on a business-semantics decision (is margin reported per-unit or per-batch?) and would need to land consistently across both views — flagged as the next batch rather than silently changed in an unattended run.

## 2026-07-12 (continuous run, part 17)

- Summary: Made the estimated **margin** and **suggested sale price** on the PrintJobs `Details` and `Index` surfaces per-unit-correct for multi-unit (batch) jobs — the "next recommended task" flagged in part 16. Both views previously computed `estimatedMargin = productSalePrice(per-unit) − estimatedTotalProductionCost(whole batch)` and `suggestedSalePrice = estimatedTotalProductionCost × (1 + targetMargin%)`, which understates margin and inflates the suggested price whenever `UnitsProduced > 1`, because `Product.SalePrice` / `Product.TargetMarginPercentage` are per-unit attributes but the cost basis was the whole batch. The fix compares against the **per-unit** cost (`estimatedTotalProductionCost ÷ UnitsProduced`, the value already displayed in the "Custo por unidade" row): `margin = salePrice − costPerUnit` and `suggested = costPerUnit × (1 + targetMargin%)`. Centralized the per-unit economics into a new pure static helper `PrintJobCostPresentation` (in the existing `ThreeDManager.Web.Presentation` namespace, mirroring the `*Presentation` pattern) with `EstimatedCostPerUnit`, `EstimatedMargin`, `EstimatedMarginPercentage`, and `SuggestedSalePrice`, so the identical math is no longer duplicated across the two views. For single-unit jobs (the default `UnitsProduced = 1`) the per-unit cost equals the whole-batch cost, so every existing single-unit costing assertion is unchanged (byte-identical). In `Details`, the two margin/suggested-price breakdown captions now read `custo por unidade` for multi-unit jobs and keep `custo total estimado` for single-unit jobs (a `costBasisLabel` conditional); `Index`'s captions never referenced the cost basis, so only the value formulas changed there. No schema, migration, or persisted data touched.

## 2026-07-13 (continuous run, part 19)

- Summary: Fixed a stock-ledger data-integrity inconsistency in the manual **AdjustStock** flows for both Materials and Products. The POST `AdjustStock` action supports three modes — `Add`, `Remove`, `Set`. `Add`/`Remove` can never resolve to a zero-quantity movement because the view-model quantity `Range` enforces a strictly positive amount (`0.01+` g / `1+` un.). But `Set` (definir estoque exato) to the value the item *already* holds resolves to `movementQuantity == 0`, and the code still persisted a `ManualAdjustment` `MaterialStockMovement`/`ProductStockMovement` row with `QuantityGrams`/`QuantityUnits == 0` and `StockBefore == StockAfter` — a misleading "an adjustment happened" ledger entry for a no-op, which also surfaced as a confusing "0 g" / "0 un." row in the Dashboard recent-movements widgets. This was inconsistent with the `Edit` flow, which already guards its inline movement with `if (stockBefore != stockAfter)`. The fix adds the same guard to both `AdjustStock` POST actions: when `movementQuantity == 0` it skips creating the movement (and the `SaveChanges` write), sets an accurate `TempData["SuccessMessage"]` ("O estoque informado já é o atual; nenhum ajuste foi registrado."), and redirects to `Details` as before. Non-no-op `Set` adjustments (and all `Add`/`Remove`) are byte-for-byte unchanged. No schema, migration, view, or persisted-data change.
- Timestamp: 2026-07-13 (continuous run, part 19)
- Files changed: `src/ThreeDManager.Web/Controllers/MaterialsController.cs`, `src/ThreeDManager.Web/Controllers/ProductsController.cs`, `tests/ThreeDManager.Tests/StockAdjustmentControllerIntegrationTests.cs` (new), `docs/context/tasks_diary.md`.
- Validation run: environment precheck passed (`threedmanager-db` was already `Up 8 hours`; `pg_isready` accepted connections). `dotnet build ThreeDManager.slnx` passed with 0 warnings / 0 errors. `dotnet test ThreeDManager.slnx` passed with all 105 tests (101 prior + 4 new), 0 failed / 0 skipped. The 4 new DB-backed HTTP-level integration tests seed a real material (500 g) / product (12 un.), GET the `AdjustStock` form for the anti-forgery token, then POST a `Set`: (a) `Materials_/Products_AdjustStock_SetToCurrentValue_...` post the current value and assert the response redirects to `/…/Details/{id}`, the stock is unchanged, and **zero** movement rows exist — proving the no-op guard; (b) `Materials_/Products_AdjustStock_SetToDifferentValue_...` post a different value and assert the stock updates and exactly one movement row is written with the correct signed `Quantity`, `StockBefore`, and `StockAfter` — proving genuine adjustments are untouched. Because the change is confined to controller logic and is fully exercised end-to-end through the real MVC pipeline (seeded DB → GET form → POST → assert persisted ledger/stock state), no separate `dotnet run` smoke was required. First test run surfaced a test-only bug (`Uri.AbsolutePath` throws on the relative redirect `Location`); fixed the assertion to compare `Location?.ToString()` and re-ran green.

A focused fix would make the quantity validation conditional on `AdjustmentType` (allow 0 only for `Set`) via `IValidatableObject` on each view-model, with tests for `Set → 0` accepted and `Add/Remove → 0` still rejected. Alternatively, the remote HTTPS ingress work (Tailscale/Cloudflare + Windows service publish/update, open since part 10) remains blocked on an elevated PowerShell session and the operator credential, so it cannot be completed unattended; and energy estimation still needs a new `Printer.PowerConsumptionWatts` schema field, so it remains out of scope for a single small batch without explicit schema-change sign-off.

## 2026-07-13 (continuous run, part 20)

- Summary: Fixed the `Set`-mode usability gap flagged as the "next recommended task" in part 19 — the manual **AdjustStock** quantity validation blocked setting stock to exactly **0** via "Definir estoque exato" (e.g. "o material acabou, corrigir o saldo para 0"), even though 0 is a valid target count. The unconditional `[Range(0.01, …)]` on `MaterialStockAdjustmentViewModel.QuantityGrams` and `[Range(1, …)]` on `ProductStockAdjustmentViewModel.QuantityUnits` rejected 0 for every mode, but the strictly-positive rule only makes sense for `Add`/`Remove` (which operate on a delta); `Set` writes an absolute target. Replaced the flat `[Range]`/quantity-`[Required]` attributes with per-mode `IValidatableObject.Validate` on both view-models: `Set` accepts `quantity >= 0` (rejecting only negatives), while `Add`/`Remove` still require `>= 0.01` g / `>= 1` un. The client-side `min="0.01"`/`min="1"` on the two `AdjustStock` views was relaxed to `min="0"` so the browser no longer blocks `Set → 0`; the real per-mode rule is enforced server-side by the new validators. Behavior for non-zero `Add`/`Remove`/`Set` is unchanged. Note the interaction with the part-19 no-op guard: `Set → 0` when stock is already 0 resolves to `movementQuantity == 0` and is still absorbed by that guard (no ledger row); `Set → 0` when stock > 0 now correctly records a single negative `ManualAdjustment` movement zeroing the stock. No schema, migration, or persisted-data change.

## 2026-07-13 (continuous run, part 25)

- Summary: Implemented the **per-mode client-side hint on the AdjustStock forms**, the "next recommended task" flagged in part 24 — a small view-only, progressive-enhancement UX increment that mirrors the existing server-side validation rule (`Set` accepts 0; `Add`/`Remove` require a strictly positive quantity — see `MaterialStockAdjustmentViewModel.Validate` / `ProductStockAdjustmentViewModel.Validate`, and the `StockAdjustmentType` constants) in the quantity input's `min` attribute and its help text as the `Tipo de ajuste` select changes. Previously both `AdjustStock` views hard-coded `min="0"` on the quantity input and a single static help sentence ("Para 'Definir estoque exato', informe o novo saldo total...") regardless of the selected mode, so the UI silently allowed typing 0 for `Add`/`Remove` (which the server then rejects) and gave no mode-specific guidance. Two symmetric edits, one per view (`Views/Materials/AdjustStock.cshtml`, `Views/Products/AdjustStock.cshtml`): (1) changed the input's static default `min` to the Add-default (`0.01` grams / `1` unit) and made the help `<div>` addressable via `id="quantityHelp"` with an Add-default sentence; (2) appended a tiny inline `<script>` in the existing `@section Scripts` block that, on load and on every `change` of `#AdjustmentType`, sets `min` to `0` + "Pode ser 0 para zerar o estoque." for `Set`, or to the positive step (`0.01`/`1`) + "(maior que zero)." for `Add`/`Remove`. The script references the centralized `@StockAdjustmentType.Set`/`.Remove` constants (compiled to the literal option values) so the client cannot drift from the server enum, guards on element presence (`if (!type || !qty || !help) return;`), and is pure presentation — the server-side `IValidatableObject` rules remain the source of truth, so a client with JS disabled sees no behavior regression.
- Timestamp: 2026-07-13 (continuous run, part 25)

Because the change is a client-side JS toggle with a genuinely observable DOM effect, ran a focused browser smoke against the real running app (`dotnet run` on 127.0.0.1:5901 with throwaway `AlphaAccess__*` creds supplied via environment only — never written to disk or committed; logged in, then drove each real served form): on **Materials/AdjustStock** for a seeded material, selecting `Set` switched the quantity input to `min="0"` and the help to "…Pode ser 0 para zerar o estoque.", while `Add` and `Remove` both showed `min="0.01"` and the "…(maior que zero)." text; on **Products/AdjustStock** for a seeded product, `Set` → `min="0"` and the units "…Pode ser 0…" text, `Add`/`Remove` → `min="1"` and "…(maior que zero)." — with no console errors.
